Confidence high.The table titled **“TIM5 alternate function remapping(1)”** lists the field **TIM5CH4_IREMAP** with two possible values 0 and 1. This indicates that the field occupies a single binary position, i.e., its width is one bit. The AFIO_MAPR register diagram also treats TIM5CH4_IREMAP as a single‑bit entry. Therefore, the statement that the bit width of the **tim5ch4_iremap** field is 1 is correct.

```json
{
    "is_true": true,
    "confidence_score": 0.97
}
```

The surrounding description of the register bits lists the access type as **rw** (read‑write) for all the remap bits, including USART1_REMAP. Therefore the field **usart1_remap** in the **mapr** register is indeed read‑write.

    "confidence_score": 0.98

The AFIO_MAPR register description shown in the search results lists the **USART1_REMAP** field among many other remap fields. In the STM32 reference manual this field is a single‑bit flag (it is either 0 = no remap or 1 = remap), i.e., its bit width is 1. There is no indication anywhere that this field occupies 52 bits; a 52‑bit field would be far larger than the 32‑bit register itself. Therefore the statement that the **usart1_remap** field has a bit width of 52 is incorrect.

```

```  

This means the **offset relative to the peripheral base address** is:

* BCR1 → 0 × 8 = 0x00  
* BCR2 → 1 × 8 = **0x08**  
* BCR3 → 2 × 8 = 0x10  
* BCR4 → 3 × 8 = 0x18
